# Enterprise Life & Annuity (L&A) Insurance Business Ontology

**Document Version**: 1.0 (Enterprise AI Agent Training & Semantic Knowledge Layer)  
**Industry Reference Benchmark**: US Life, Annuity & Institutional Retirement Carriers (Corebridge Financial, Nuveen, Athene, Empower, Lincoln Financial, Nationwide, Pacific Life, Prudential, MetLife, New York Life, MassMutual, Jackson National, Equitable, Securian, Symetra, Principal Financial Group)  
**Purpose**: This document establishes the authoritative semantic business ontology, structural entity-relationship semantics, regulatory mechanics, and SQL/data-product generation guardrails required to train autonomous AI data engineering, actuarial analytics, and business intelligence agents operating over the enterprise Life & Annuity (L&A) Logical and Physical Data Models.

---



## 1. Enterprise L&A Data Domain Ontology Definitions (All 16 Domains)



### 1.1 Domain 1: Product

The **Product** domain serves as the foundational master catalog, regulatory rulebook, and structural blueprint for every marketable Life Insurance contract and Annuity offering designed, filed, and distributed by the enterprise. Across the US Life and Annuity industry—spanning institutional retirement leaders like Corebridge Financial, Empower, Nationwide, Voya, and Principal, as well as retail annuity and life powerhouses such as Athene, Lincoln Financial, Jackson National, Pacific Life, Prudential, New York Life, MassMutual, and Equitable—no policy or group certificate can be quoted, underwritten, issued, or valued without inheriting its governing rules from the Product domain. Architecturally, this domain is organized across six core logical entities: `Product Line`, `Product`, `Product State Filing & Availability`, `Product Crediting & Liquidity Rule`, `Product Eligible Investment Fund`, and `Product Eligible Rider`.

At the top of the hierarchy, `Product Line` categorizes offerings by statutory NAIC Annual Statement ("Blue Book") line of business (`Line of Business Code`), primary Internal Revenue Code tax regime (`Regulatory Tax Qualification Type` such as IRC 403(b), 401(a), 457(b), Traditional/Roth IRA, Non-Qualified Annuity under IRC Section 72, or Life Insurance under IRC Section 7702), and `Target Market Segment` (Higher Education & Research, Healthcare & Non-Profit, K-12 Public Education, Retail Wealth Management, and High Net Worth Estate Planning). Beneath each product line, the `Product` entity defines the exact actuarial, legal, and operational parameters of each contract form (`Product Identifier`, `Product Code`, `Product Name`, `Product Category`), distinguishing between General Account Fixed Annuities (such as Institutional Retirement Annuity [RA], Group Retirement Annuity [GRA], Retirement Choice [RC], and Multi-Year Guaranteed Annuities [MYGAs]), Registered Separate Account Variable Annuities (such as CREF Variable Retirement Annuities), Fixed Indexed Annuities (FIAs), Registered Index-Linked Annuities (RILAs), Single/Deferred Payout Annuities (SPIAs/DIAs), and Life Insurance designs (Term Life, Whole Life, Universal Life [UL], Indexed Universal Life [IUL], and Variable Universal Life [VUL]).

Furthermore, the Product domain enforces strict state-by-state jurisdictional compliance via `Product State Filing & Availability`, tracking SERFF and Interstate Insurance Product Regulation Commission (IIPRC) approvals alongside extraterritorial state variations such as New York Department of Financial Services (NY DFS) versus non-NY companion carriers (`Issuing Legal Company Code`). It governs General Account guaranteed interest rate floors and withdrawal restrictions through `Product Crediting & Liquidity Rule`—capturing institutional liquidity constructs like institutional lifetime-income-only RA rules, 10-year Transfer Payout Annuity (TPA) installments, and 84-month Retirement Choice systematic withdrawals—while `Product Eligible Investment Fund` and `Product Eligible Rider` act as associative rule tables constraining which sub-accounts and supplemental guarantees can legally attach to each base product. For AI agents, the Product domain is the primary dimension for slicing enterprise Assets Under Management (AUM), statutory reserves, net flows, and new business production by product mechanics, balance sheet backing (`General or Separate Account Indicator`), and regulatory perimeter.

### 1.2 Domain 2: Policy (Contract & Certificate Administration)

The **Policy** domain represents the operational heart of the insurance carrier's core administration ecosystem, housing the master contract and group certificate records for every Life Insurance policy and Annuity contract ever issued, administered, or terminated across the enterprise. In major US L&A organizations—which frequently operate heterogeneous policy administration systems (`Source Administration System Code`) such as OMNI for institutional retirement plans, ALIP or Vantage-One for life insurance, and FAST or BaNCS for retail annuities—the Policy domain provides the unified enterprise system of record across four core logical entities: `Policy`, `Policy Coverage Segment`, `Policy Vintage Bucket Balance`, and `Policy Loan`. Every record in the `Policy` entity (`Policy Identifier`, `Policy Number`, `Contract Certificate Number`) instantiates a specific `Product Identifier` and optionally links to an employer-sponsored `Institutional Plan Identifier` when issued as an institutional retirement plan certificate (such as an institutional Retirement Choice Plus certificate or a 403(b) individual annuity contract).

The `Policy` entity tracks the complete contractual lifecycle through `Policy Status` (`Pending Issue`, `Free Look`, `Active in Accumulation`, `Active in Payout / Annuitized`, `Paid-Up`, `Grace Period`, `Surrendered`, `Lapsed`, `Death Claim Pending`, `Terminated`, `Closed`), alongside critical financial valuation balances including `Face Amount` (for life contracts), `Current Total Contract Value` (gross accumulation across fixed and variable accounts), `Current Cash Surrender Value` (net liquid value after surrender charges, Market Value Adjustments [MVA], and outstanding policy loans), and `Cost Basis Amount` (cumulative after-tax investment in the contract under IRC Section 72). Because complex life and annuity contracts are rarely monolithic, `Policy Coverage Segment` decomposes a single policy into distinct actuarial layers (`Coverage Sequence Number`, `Coverage Type`) such as base life coverage, supplemental level term riders, paid-up additions (PUA) layers from whole life dividends, or separate employee versus employer-match coverage segments, each carrying its own `Coverage Benefit Amount` and underwritten `Mortality Risk Class`.

Crucially, the Policy domain models two specialized balance-sheet mechanisms essential to US institutional and retail carriers. First, `Policy Vintage Bucket Balance` supports participating general account vintage accounting, where participant contributions are grouped into specific calendar contribution windows (`Vintage Period Identifier`, `Contribution Start Date`, `Contribution End Date`) so that each vintage tranche earns its own guaranteed minimum interest rate (`Guaranteed Minimum Interest Rate`) plus Board-declared excess interest (`Current Declared Total Crediting Rate`) based on the fixed-income yields prevailing when the money was invested. Second, `Policy Loan` manages collateralized loans taken against life insurance cash values or qualified retirement plan accumulations, enforcing IRS Section 72(p) statutory borrowing limits ($50,000 or 50% of vested balance), tracking `Outstanding Principal Balance` and `Accrued Loan Interest Amount`, and flagging delinquent loans that trigger taxable `1099-R` deemed distributions (`Loan Default Status`). AI agents querying in-force metrics, net surrender exposure, or policy counts must treat the Policy domain as the central fact/dimension hub connecting customers, products, funds, riders, and transactions.

### 1.3 Domain 3: Riders (Supplemental Benefits & Living/Death Guarantees)

The **Riders** domain governs the catalog, policy-level attachment, and complex shadow-account valuation of optional and embedded supplemental benefit guarantees that modify or enhance the risk profile of a base Life Insurance or Annuity contract. Across the US retail and institutional annuity landscape—particularly at variable, indexed, and RILA carriers such as Jackson National, Lincoln Financial, Nationwide, Allianz, Prudential, Equitable, Pacific Life, and Principal—supplemental living and death benefit riders are the primary value proposition driving trillions of dollars of retirement savings, while simultaneously representing the most capital-intensive, market-sensitive liabilities on an insurer's balance sheet. This domain is structured around three tightly integrated logical entities: `Rider Product Definition`, `Policy Rider Coverage`, and `Rider Benefit Base Valuation`.

`Rider Product Definition` serves as the master specification table for all supplemental benefits (`Rider Product Identifier`, `Rider Code`, `Rider Name`, `Rider Category`), encompassing Guaranteed Minimum Withdrawal Benefits (GMWB) and Guaranteed Lifetime Withdrawal Benefits (GLWB) that guarantee a lifetime income stream even if market downturns or longevity exhaust the underlying contract account value; Guaranteed Minimum Income Benefits (GMIB) that guarantee a favorable annuitization purchase floor; Guaranteed Minimum Accumulation Benefits (GMAB) that protect principal over a holding period; and Guaranteed Minimum Death Benefits (GMDB) that protect beneficiaries via Return of Premium (ROP), Maximum Anniversary Value (MAV) high-water-mark step-ups, or compound roll-ups. On the life insurance side, it defines Waiver of Premium / Monthly Deduction disability riders, Accelerated Death Benefit (ADB) riders for terminal or chronic illness / Long-Term Care (under IRC Sections 101(g) and 7702B), and Cost of Living Adjustment (COLA) inflation riders. It also captures pricing parameters such as `Rider Fee Calculation Basis`, `Annual Step-Up Rate`, and `Waiting Period Years`.

When a policyholder elects (or receives an embedded) rider, `Policy Rider Coverage` links the `Rider Product Identifier` to the `Policy Identifier`, tracking its operational `Rider Status` (`Active in Deferral / Roll-Up`, `Active - Exercised in Lifetime Withdrawal Phase`, `Terminated due to Excess Withdrawal`), `Rider Effective Date`, and `Current Annual Rider Charge Rate`. Because living and death benefit riders operate on contractual "shadow bases" that diverge from actual cash value, `Rider Benefit Base Valuation` records periodic point-in-time snapshots of the policy's `Guaranteed Benefit Base Amount`, `High Water Mark Value`, and `Roll-Up Base Amount`, alongside the age-tiered `Maximum Annual Lifetime Withdrawal Percentage`, `Remaining Guaranteed Annual Withdrawal Amount`, and any `Excess Withdrawal Reduction Amount`. For AI agents, the single most critical semantic rule in the Riders domain is recognizing that the `Guaranteed Benefit Base Amount` is a non-cashable actuarial calculation shadow base used solely to compute guaranteed lifetime withdrawal allowances or death benefit step-ups—it can never be withdrawn as a lump sum and must never be added to `Current Total Contract Value` when calculating enterprise Assets Under Management (AUM).

### 1.4 Domain 4: Fund (Separate Accounts, CREF Accounts & General Account Funds)

The **Fund** domain models the investment vehicles, daily unitized net asset valuations, policyholder allocation instructions, and sub-account position holdings that underpin variable annuities, variable universal life (VUL) policies, registered index-linked annuities (RILAs), fixed indexed annuities (FIAs), and general account investment options. In the US retirement and insurance market, carriers offer a spectrum of investment structures (`Account Structure Type`): insulated Separate Accounts registered with the SEC as Unit Investment Trusts (UITs) investing in underlying mutual fund sub-accounts (such as Nuveen, Vanguard, BlackRock, or Fidelity VIP portfolios), unique registered management investment company variable accounts like the College Retirement Equities Fund (`CREF Stock Account`, `CREF Social Choice Account`), non-unitized direct commercial property separate accounts like the `Direct Real Estate Separate Account` (equipped with a carrier-backed liquidity guarantee), indexed crediting strategies linked to the S&P 500 or MSCI EAFE, and General Account fixed options (`Participating General Account Fixed`). The Fund domain organizes these mechanics across four core entities: `Investment Fund`, `Fund Unit Price`, `Policy Fund Allocation Election`, and `Policy Valuation Account`.

The `Investment Fund` entity (`Investment Fund Identifier`, `Fund Ticker or Code`, `Fund Name`) defines each fund's `Asset Class`, `Fund Manager Name`, `Net Expense Ratio Percentage` (combining investment management fees, Mortality & Expense [M&E] risk charges, and administrative fees), and `Assumed Investment Return Rate (AIR)`. A hallmark of the institutional variable dual-unit accounting architecture—and variable annuity administration broadly—is captured in `Fund Unit Price`. Every business day at the 4:00 PM ET New York Stock Exchange market close (`Valuation Date`), the fund accounting engine calculates two distinct unit prices: the `Accumulation Unit Value (AUV)`, which measures the net asset value of a unit held by participants during the savings/accumulation phase via the `Daily Net Investment Factor`, and the `Annuity Unit Value`, which governs variable lifetime income checks during the payout phase by measuring whether actual fund investment performance exceeded or trailed the hurdle `Assumed Investment Return Rate (AIR)` (commonly 4.00% in CREF).

At the contract level, `Policy Fund Allocation Election` stores the policyholder's standing percentage splits (`Allocation Percentage` summing to 100% per `Allocation Type`) for future employee contributions, employer matches, automatic quarterly rebalancing, and Dollar Cost Averaging (DCA). Finally, `Policy Valuation Account` (`Holding Account Identifier`) records the policy's actual point-in-time position in each fund, tracking `Valuation Phase` (`Accumulation Phase` vs. `Annuitized Lifetime Payout Phase`), exact fractional `Number of Accumulation Units Held` or `Number of Annuity Units Held`, `Current Market Value Amount`, `Surrender Free Value Amount`, and optional linkage to `Policy Vintage Bucket Identifier` for vintage-bucketed general account holdings. AI agents building portfolio exposure, asset class concentration, or fund flow data products rely on the Fund domain to bridge policy-level balances to underlying capital market assets.

### 1.5 Domain 5: Transactions (Policy Financial & Non-Financial Activity)

The **Transactions** domain serves as the immutable event ledger and operational audit trail for all monetary movements and administrative contract modifications occurring across the lifecycle of a Policy. In US Life and Annuity administration, every change in a policy's accumulation value, unit balance, loan balance, or contractual configuration must be driven by a discrete, timestamped transaction event to satisfy SEC Rule 22c-1 forward-pricing mandates, FINRA books-and-records rules, NAIC market conduct audits, SOX financial controls, and IRS tax reporting. This domain comprises three primary logical entities: `Financial Transaction`, `Transaction Fund Allocation Detail`, and `Non-Financial Service Transaction`.

`Financial Transaction` (`Financial Transaction Identifier`, `Transaction Reference Number`) captures the header-level monetary event posted to a `Policy Identifier` (and optionally linked to an institutional `Remittance Batch Identifier`). Through `Transaction Type`, it classifies inflows (such as `Employee Pre-Tax Contribution`, `Designated Roth Contribution`, `Employer Matching Contribution`, `Direct Rollover In`, and `IRC 1035 Tax-Free Exchange In`), internal movements (`Internal Fund-to-Fund Transfer`, `Transfer Payout Annuity (TPA) Installment`), outflows (`Systematic Withdrawal`, `Required Minimum Distribution (RMD)`, `Partial Surrender`, `Full Surrender`, `Lifetime Annuity Income Payment`, `Policy Loan Disbursement`, `Death Benefit Payout`), and internal contract charges (`GMWB Rider Fee Deduction`, `Monthly Cost of Insurance (COI) Deduction`). Crucially, the entity tracks three distinct dates—`Transaction Effective Date` (contractual as-of date for interest/tax), `Transaction Processing Date` (system execution date), and `Valuation Price Date` (the market close date whose `Accumulation Unit Value (AUV)` was applied when In-Good-Order [IGO] instructions arrived prior to the 4:00 PM ET cutoff)—alongside the full gross-to-net financial waterfall: `Gross Transaction Amount` minus `Surrender Charge Deducted Amount`, plus/minus `Market Value Adjustment (MVA) Amount`, minus `Federal Tax Withheld Amount` and `State Tax Withheld Amount`, equaling `Net Transaction Amount`. It also manages backouts and NSF check reversals via `Transaction Reversal Indicator`.

Because a single financial transaction (such as a $2,000 payroll contribution or a $10,000 rebalancing transfer) typically spans multiple investment funds or general account vintages, `Transaction Fund Allocation Detail` breaks the parent transaction into granular fund-level debit and credit lines (`Allocated Dollar Amount`, `Unit Price Applied`, `Units Purchased or Redeemed Count`, and `Crediting Rate Vintage Assigned`), updating the corresponding `Holding Account Identifier` in `Policy Valuation Account`. Complementing the monetary ledger, `Non-Financial Service Transaction` (`Service Transaction Identifier`, `Endorsement Type`) logs non-monetary contract maintenance events—such as beneficiary changes, future contribution allocation changes, ownership transfers, address updates, and smoker-to-non-smoker reclassifications—across self-service web, mobile, advisor workbench, and contact center channels (`Processed By Channel`). AI agents calculating net cash flows, organic growth, surrender rates, or fee revenue must always filter `Transaction Reversal Indicator` and distinguish external cash inflows/outflows from internal fund-to-fund transfers.

### 1.6 Domain 6: Reinsurance (Ceded & Assumed Risk Management)

The **Reinsurance** domain governs the transfer of mortality, longevity, morbidity, and capital-market tail risks from the direct writing insurance carrier (the ceding company) to external third-party professional reinsurers (such as RGA, Swiss Re, Munich Re, SCOR, and Hannover Re) or affiliated offshore/onshore captive and sidecar reinsurers (such as Athene Co-Invest Reinsurance Affiliate [ACRA], Prismic Life Re, or Martello Re). In the capital-intensive US Life and Annuity industry, reinsurance is a vital strategic lever used to protect statutory surplus against catastrophic or large-case mortality claims, manage longevity risk on institutional payout annuity blocks, finance new business strain (XXX/AXXX and PBR reserves), and optimize Risk-Based Capital (RBC). The domain is structured across five logical entities: `Reinsurer Counterparty`, `Reinsurance Treaty`, `Reinsurance Treaty Participant`, `Reinsured Policy Cession`, and `Reinsurance Claim Recovery`.

`Reinsurer Counterparty` (`Reinsurer Counterparty Identifier`) maintains the legal, regulatory, and credit risk profile of each assuming company, including its five-digit `NAIC Company Code` (required for NAIC Annual Statement Schedule S filings), `Federal Tax Identification Number`, `Credit Rating (AM Best / S&P)`, `Domicile Country`, and critical statutory authorization status (`Authorized vs Unauthorized Reinsurer Status` and `Collateral Trust Required Indicator`). Under US state statutory accounting principles (SAP), if a reinsurer is unauthorized and not a Certified Reciprocal Jurisdiction Reinsurer in the ceding company's state of domicile (e.g., New York Regulation 20 / Regulation 114), the ceding carrier cannot take a statutory reserve credit on its balance sheet unless the reinsurer posts qualifying collateral via a Regulation 114 trust, clean irrevocable Letter of Credit (LOC), or Funds Withheld structure.

`Reinsurance Treaty` (`Reinsurance Treaty Identifier`, `Treaty Number`, `Treaty Name`) codifies the formal risk-transfer contract, specifying the `Treaty Structure Type`: `Yearly Renewable Term (YRT - Mortality Only)`, where only the Net Amount at Risk (NAR) is ceded on an annual mortality rate scale; `Coinsurance`, where the reinsurer assumes a proportional quota share of premiums, benefits, and statutory reserves; `Modified Coinsurance (ModCo)` or `Funds Withheld Coinsurance`, where the ceding carrier retains physical custody of the backing assets and reserves to avoid collateral friction while transferring economic investment and insurance performance; or `Longevity Risk Swap` treaties on payout annuities. It defines `Automatic vs Facultative Indicator` underwriting rules, `Carrier Retention Limit Amount` (e.g., retaining up to $2.5M per life), and `Cession Percentage`, while `Reinsurance Treaty Participant` resolves multi-reinsurer syndicate shares (`Reinsurer Share Percentage`). At the policy level, `Reinsured Policy Cession` tracks monthly/quarterly bordereau exposures (`Direct Face Amount`, `Retained Risk Amount`, `Ceded Net Amount at Risk (NAR)`, `Ceded Reserve Amount`, `Reinsurance Premium Due Amount`, `Expense Allowance Amount`), and `Reinsurance Claim Recovery` manages the billing and cash collection (`Reinsurance Recoverable Amount`, `Recovery Settlement Status`) when a reinsured policy incurs a claim, monitoring statutory 90-day aging rules for overdue recoverables.

### 1.7 Domain 7: Finance (Insurance Sub-Ledger, General Ledger & Investment Accounting)

The **Finance** domain serves as the enterprise accounting backbone and multi-basis financial reporting engine for the Life and Annuity organization, bridging granular policy administration transactions, actuarial reserve movements, reinsurance settlements, and general account investment returns into auditable financial statements. Unlike standard corporate enterprises that maintain a single accounting book, a US Life and Annuity insurance group must simultaneously maintain and reconcile three distinct regulatory accounting bases (`Accounting Basis`): **US GAAP** (governed by FASB ASC 944, including Long-Duration Targeted Improvements [LDTI] for public/GAAP filers), **Statutory Accounting Principles (SAP)** (governed by the NAIC Accounting Practices and Procedures Manual and reported on the quarterly/annual NAIC "Blue Book" statement to state insurance commissioners, with major New York-domiciled carriers publishing audited statutory financial statements under NY DFS oversight), and **US Federal Income Tax Accounting** (governed by Internal Revenue Code Subchapter L, Sections 801–818). The Finance domain is modeled across five core entities: `General Ledger Account`, `Accounting Period`, `Sub-Ledger Journal Entry`, `Journal Entry Line`, and `General Account Crediting Rate Declaration`.

`General Ledger Account` (`General Ledger Account Identifier`, `GL Account Number`, `GL Account Name`) defines the enterprise Chart of Accounts across `Account Classification` (`Asset`, `Liability`, `Policyholder Surplus / Equity`, `Revenue`, `Expense`, `Contra-Asset / Valuation Allowance`), while strictly segregating balance sheets via `Separate vs General Account Ledger Code` (`General Account Ledger`, `Separate Account Ledger`, `Corporate Holding Ledger`) so that insulated Separate Account assets and liabilities (e.g., CREF or variable sub-accounts) are never commingled with the carrier's General Account backing fixed guarantees. `Accounting Period` (`Accounting Period Identifier`, `Fiscal Year`, `Fiscal Month or Quarter`, `Period Close Status`) enforces SOX financial close cutoffs across open, soft-close (actuarial adjustment only), and hard-closed periods.

Whenever a policy transaction occurs in `Financial Transaction` or a valuation run completes in `Actuarial Valuation Reserve`, the insurance sub-ledger accounting rules engine generates a `Sub-Ledger Journal Entry` (`Sub-Ledger Journal Entry Identifier`, `Journal Entry Number`, `Accounting Event Type`) containing balanced debit and credit rows in `Journal Entry Line` (`Debit Amount`, `Credit Amount`). Each journal line maps directly to a `General Ledger Account Identifier`, an optional GAAP `LDTI Cohort Identifier` for ASC 944 roll-forward disclosures, and a `Statutory Annual Statement Line Reference` (e.g., Summary of Operations Page 4 Line 1, Exhibit 5 Aggregate Reserves, or Exhibit 8 Claim Liabilities). Additionally, `General Account Crediting Rate Declaration` records Board-approved interest crediting schedules (`Guaranteed Base Interest Rate` plus `Additional Declared Dividend or Excess Interest Rate` equaling `Total Effective Crediting Rate`) for each product and vintage period, governing how general account portfolio investment income is shared with policyholders. AI agents building financial P&L, balance sheet, or statutory audit products must always filter by `Accounting Basis` and ensure total debits equal total credits across every journal entry.

### 1.8 Domain 8: Experience Studies (Historical Behavioral & Biometric Analytics)

The **Experience Studies** domain provides the empirical actuarial feedback loop that transforms historical policy administration, transaction, and claim records into statistically credible biometric and policyholder behavioral insights. In a Life and Annuity insurance carrier, product pricing, statutory Principles-Based Reserving (PBR under VM-20, VM-21, and VM-22), and US GAAP LDTI annual assumption unlocking depend entirely on accurately measuring how actual policyholder mortality, longevity, lapse, and benefit utilization compare against industry benchmark tables and current valuation assumptions. This domain is structured across three core logical entities: `Experience Study`, `Experience Exposure & Decrement Cohort`, and the associative policy-year observation entity `Experience Study Policy Observation`.

`Experience Study` (`Experience Study Identifier`, `Study Name`, `Study Type`) catalogs formal actuarial investigations conducted over a multi-year historical observation window (`Observation Start Date` to `Observation End Date`, typically excluding a recent 3-to-6-month lag buffer to prevent distortion from Incurred But Not Reported [IBNR] claims). Across US L&A carriers like New York Life, Prudential, Corebridge, and Lincoln Financial, experience studies cover six primary decrement types: `Life Mortality Study` (measuring death rates $q_x$ on life insurance blocks against benchmarks like the `2015 Valuation Basic Table (2015 VBT)`), `Annuitant Longevity / Mortality Study` (measuring survival and mortality of payout annuitants against benchmarks like the `2012 Individual Annuity Mortality (2012 IAM) Basic` table or the `Pri-2012 Higher Education Retiree Mortality Table`, where university faculty exhibit significantly longer life expectancy than the general population), `Voluntary Lapse & Surrender Study` (measuring baseline surrenders and shock lapses at the end of surrender charge periods), `Deferred-to-Payout Annuitization Election Rate Study` (crucial for valuing Participating General Account Fixed vintage guarantees), `GMWB / GMIB Living Benefit Rider Utilization Study`, and `Maintenance Expense Unit Cost Study`.

At the granular policy-year level, `Experience Study Policy Observation` (`Policy Observation Identifier`) links each historical `Policy Identifier` (and any resulting `Claim Identifier`) to the study, calculating the exact `Exposure Fraction Years` (between `0.0000` and `1.0000`) that the contract was active and exposed to the studied risk during the observation year, along with the binary `Decrement Indicator` (`1` if the event occurred, `0` if the policy survived/persisted). These policy-year observations are rolled up into `Experience Exposure & Decrement Cohort` (`Experience Cohort Identifier`), which segments the experience across key actuarial risk dimensions: `Product Category`, `Attained Age Band`, `Policy Duration Year`, `Gender`, `Smoker Status`, `Tax Qualification Type`, and living benefit `In-The-Moneyness Band` ($Benefit\ Base / Account\ Value$). For each cell, the cohort stores `Total Policies Exposed Count`, `Total Face or Account Value Exposed Amount`, `Actual Decrement Count`, `Actual Decrement Dollar Amount`, `Expected Decrement Count`, and the critical `Actual-to-Expected (A/E) Ratio`. AI agents querying experience studies must never average cohort-level `Actual-to-Expected (A/E) Ratio` values using a simple unweighted average; they must always compute weighted A/E ratios as $\sum \text{Actual Decrements} / \sum \text{Expected Decrements}$ by count or dollar amount.

### 1.9 Domain 9: Actuarial (Valuation, Reserves, Pricing & Capital Modeling)

The **Actuarial** domain encompasses the mathematical valuation models, governance-locked assumption sets, statutory and GAAP reserve liabilities, Long-Duration Targeted Improvements (LDTI) cohorts, Risk-Based Capital (RBC) requirements, and stochastic cash flow projections that quantify the insurer's long-term contractual obligations. Because Life Insurance policies and Annuity contracts represent multi-decade promises—often spanning 30 to 60 years from a participant's first 403(b) payroll contribution at age 25 through lifetime annuity payouts past age 95—policy benefit reserves constitute the single largest liability on a Life & Annuity carrier's balance sheet. This domain is organized across four core logical entities: `Actuarial Assumption Set`, `LDTI Valuation Cohort`, `Actuarial Valuation Reserve`, and `Actuarial Cash Flow Projection`.

`Actuarial Assumption Set` (`Actuarial Assumption Set Identifier`, `Assumption Set Name`, `Assumption Version Number`) stores the SOX-controlled, Appointed-Actuary-approved (`Governance Approval Status`) parameters feeding actuarial modeling engines (such as FIS Prophet, Moody's AXIS, or Milliman Integrate/MG-Alfa). Linked directly to the calibrating `Experience Study Identifier`, each assumption set specifies its `Valuation Purpose` (`US GAAP LDTI (ASC 944 Best Estimate)`, `Statutory Principles-Based Reserving (VM-20 / VM-21 / VM-22)`, `Asset Adequacy Analysis / Cash Flow Testing (CFT)`, or `New Product Pricing & Profitability`), alongside the `Base Mortality or Longevity Table`, two-dimensional `Mortality Improvement Scale` (e.g., `SOA Scale MP-2021` or `NAIC Scale G2`), `Dynamic Lapse Formula Type` (which dynamically lowers lapse rates when a GMWB rider is deeply in-the-money and raises lapse rates when market interest rates spike above fixed crediting rates), `Discount Rate Curve Reference`, and `Assumed Annuitization Utilization Rate`.

For US GAAP reporting under FASB ASC 944, `LDTI Valuation Cohort` (`LDTI Cohort Identifier`, `Product Issue Year`, `Product Grouping Name`) aggregates long-duration contracts into annual issue-year cohorts to track the Liability for Future Policy Benefits (LFPB) using both the `Locked-In Discount Rate` (for net income recognition) and the `Current Market Discount Rate` (Single-A corporate bond yield for balance sheet remeasurement through Other Comprehensive Income [OCI]), alongside constant-level `Deferred Acquisition Cost (DAC) Balance` amortization and fair-value `Market Risk Benefit (MRB) Fair Value` measurements for GMxB riders. At the individual policy or model-point level, `Actuarial Valuation Reserve` (`Actuarial Valuation Reserve Identifier`) stores period-end reserve calculations by `Valuation Basis`—capturing Statutory PBR components (`Net Premium Reserve Amount`, `Deterministic Reserve Amount`, `Stochastic Reserve Amount` at CTE 70, `Future Policy Benefit Reserve Amount`, and `Additional Actuarial Reserve (AAR) Amount` held to prefund high guaranteed Participating General Account Fixed vintage payout rates), plus `Net Amount at Risk (NAR)` and `Required Risk-Based Capital (RBC) Amount` across C-1, C-2, and C-3 risks. Finally, `Actuarial Cash Flow Projection` stores period-by-period deterministic and stochastic cash flow vectors (`Projected Premium Inflow Amount`, `Projected Death Benefit Outflow`, `Projected Annuity Income Outflow`, `Projected Surrender Outflow`, `Projected Net Investment Income`) across economic scenarios (`Economic Scenario Number`) for Asset-Liability Management (ALM).

### 1.10 Domain 10: Agent & Agency (Distribution, Wealth Management & Compensation)

The **Agent & Agency** domain manages the multi-channel distribution ecosystem, producer licensing and regulatory appointments, policy-to-advisor relationship assignments, and commission/compensation accounting across the enterprise. In the modern US Life and Annuity industry, distribution spans diverse channels: internal salaried and bonus-eligible wealth management and institutional relationship channels (such as Institutional Wealth Management Advisors, Workplace Retirement Consultants, and Institutional Relationship Managers serving university and hospital sponsors), independent Broker-Dealers (such as LPL Financial, Raymond James, Ameriprise, and Commonwealth), wirehouses and regional banks, Independent Marketing Organizations (IMOs) and Brokerage General Agencies (BGAs), and fee-only Registered Investment Advisors (RIAs). The domain is modeled across five core logical entities: `Agency`, `Producer / Advisor`, `Producer License & Carrier Appointment`, `Policy Producer Assignment`, and `Compensation Schedule & Commission Statement`.

`Agency` (`Agency Identifier`, `Agency Name`, `Agency Type`) represents the corporate distribution firm or internal advisory division, tracking its NAIC entity `National Producer Number (NPN) - Entity`, FINRA `Broker-Dealer CRD Number` (such as `CRD-6413` for LPL Financial LLC or `CRD-20472` for Institutional Wealth & Annuity Services, LLC), and `Selling Agreement Status` (`Active - New Business & Servicing`, `Servicing Only`, `Terminated`). Beneath each agency, `Producer / Advisor` (`Producer Identifier`, `Producer Full Name`, `Producer Role Type`) holds the master profile of each individual agent, wealth advisor, or institutional relationship manager, indexed by their unique NAIC `National Producer Number (NPN)` and individual `FINRA CRD Number`.

Before any producer can solicit or sign an application in a given state, `Producer License & Carrier Appointment` (`License & Appointment Identifier`) validates strict regulatory prerequisites: an active state Department of Insurance `License Number` in the `State Jurisdiction` prior to its `License Expiration Date`, the required statutory `Line of Authority` (`Life Insurance Only` vs. `Life and Variable Contracts`), an active `Carrier Appointment Status` (including Just-In-Time [JIT] appointment rules), appropriate `FINRA Series Qualification` (`Series 6`, `Series 7`, `Series 63`, `Series 65/66`) for variable or registered index-linked products, and a verified `Product Training and Reg BI Completion Date` proving completion of state NAIC Model #275 Best Interest 4-hour training and carrier-specific product training prior to the application signature date. `Policy Producer Assignment` (`Policy Producer Assignment Identifier`) links one or more producers and agencies to a `Policy Identifier`, distinguishing `Assignment Role` (`Primary Writing Agent`, `Joint Writing Agent`, `Current Servicing Advisor (Orphan Reassigned)`, `Institutional Relationship Manager`, `General Agency Override Recipient`) and `Commission Split Percentage`. Finally, `Compensation Schedule & Commission Statement` calculates and settles upfront commissions, renewal commissions, quarterly asset-based trails, RIA advisory fee deductions, and early surrender/free-look `Chargeback Recovery Amount` clawbacks via EFT or DTCC/NSCC Commission Settlement (COMS) feeds.

### 1.11 Domain 11: Party, Customer & Household

The **Party, Customer & Household** domain functions as the enterprise Master Data Management (MDM) Customer 360 hub, resolving identity, demographic attributes, tax residency, contact preferences, household wealth relationships, and contractual policy roles for every natural person and legal entity interacting with the insurance carrier. Across large L&A enterprises, a single individual—for instance, a tenured university professor or hospital physician—frequently holds multiple institutional 403(b) and 401(a) retirement certificates, a retail rollover IRA or variable annuity, an individual universal life insurance policy, and simultaneously serves as the joint annuitant and primary beneficiary on their spouse's contracts. Without an enterprise MDM Party layer, legacy administration systems fragment that individual into disconnected silos. This domain unifies customer identity across five logical entities: `Party`, `Party Contact & Address Profile`, `Household`, `Household Member Assignment`, and `Policy Party Role`.

The `Party` entity (`Party Identifier`) establishes the single deduplicated golden record for both natural persons and non-natural organizations via `Party Type` (`Individual Person`, `Employer Sponsor Institution`, `Irrevocable Life Insurance Trust (ILIT)`, `Revocable Living Trust`, `Decedent Estate`, `Charitable Endowment / 501(c)(3) Foundation`). It stores authoritative legal identity attributes: `Full Legal Name`, `Date of Birth` (essential for issue age, mortality charges, annuity payout factors, and RMD start ages), `Date of Death` (populated via Social Security Death Master File [DMF] screening or certified death certificates), `Gender`, `Marital Status` (critical for enforcing ERISA spousal consent rules on qualified retirement plan beneficiary designations and joint-and-survivor annuity waivers), encrypted `Tax Identification Number (SSN/EIN)`, `Citizenship and Tax Residency Status` (governing IRS Form W-9 vs. Form W-8BEN / FATCA / 1042-S reporting), `Smoking or Tobacco Status`, `Occupation Class`, `Primary Residence State`, and `KYC and AML Verification Status` (USA PATRIOT Act CIP and OFAC sanctions screening).

`Party Contact & Address Profile` (`Contact Profile Identifier`) manages residential, mailing, and tax addresses alongside `Electronic Delivery Consent Indicator`, explicitly tracking `Returned Post Office (RPO - Undeliverable Address Flag)` statuses that trigger unclaimed property due diligence workflows under state escheatment laws. To empower wealth management advisors and holistic retirement planning, `Household` (`Household Identifier`, `Household Name`, `Household Tier Segment`, `Total Household Aggregated Assets`) and `Household Member Assignment` group spouses, partners, dependents, and family trusts into a unified economic unit. Finally, `Policy Party Role` (`Policy Party Role Identifier`) resolves the many-to-many relationship between `Party Identifier` and `Policy Identifier`, specifying the exact legal `Role Type` (`Policy Owner`, `Joint Policy Owner`, `Institutional Plan Participant`, `Primary Insured`, `Primary Annuitant`, `Joint Survivor Co-Annuitant`, `Primary Beneficiary`, `Contingent Beneficiary`, `Trustee`, `Payor`, `QDRO Alternate Payee`), along with `Beneficiary Share Percentage`, `Per Stirpes vs Per Capita Indicator`, and `Revocable vs Irrevocable Indicator`.

### 1.12 Domain 12: Institutional Plan & Employer Sponsor (Institutional Retirement Core Domain)

The **Institutional Plan & Employer Sponsor** domain models the B2B2C workplace retirement architecture that distinguishes institutional retirement leaders—most notably Corebridge Financial, Fidelity, Voya, Empower, Lincoln Financial, Nationwide, and Principal—from purely retail insurance carriers. In the institutional market, the insurance carrier enters into a Master Services Agreement with an employer sponsor (a private or public university, academic medical center, research institute, K-12 school district, or municipality) to administer and fund one or more workplace retirement plans. Individual employees enroll as plan participants, receiving group annuity certificates or individual custodial/annuity contracts governed simultaneously by the underlying insurance product terms AND the employer's legal plan document. This domain is structured across four core logical entities: `Employer Sponsor Organization`, `Institutional Retirement Plan`, `Plan Eligibility & Contribution Rule`, and `Plan Vesting Schedule`.

`Employer Sponsor Organization` (`Employer Sponsor Identifier`, linked to `Party Identifier`) captures the corporate profile of the sponsoring institution, including its `Employer Identification Number (EIN)`, `Institution Legal Name` (e.g., Harvard College, State University of New York [SUNY], Mayo Clinic), `Institution Sector` (`Higher Education - Private University/College`, `Higher Education - Public State University System`, `Academic Medical Center & Healthcare System`, `K-12 Public School District`, `Independent Non-Profit Research & Museum`), `Master Services Agreement Effective Date`, and the assigned `Relationship Manager Assigned`.

Beneath each employer sponsor, `Institutional Retirement Plan` (`Institutional Plan Identifier`, `Plan Number`, `Plan Official Name`) codifies the legal plan document and Summary Plan Description (SPD) rules, classifying the plan via `Plan Tax Code Type`: `403(b) ERISA Covered Plan` (common at private 501(c)(3) universities and non-profit hospitals subject to Department of Labor Form 5500 audits and ERISA fiduciary duties), `403(b) Non-ERISA Governmental / Church Plan` (common at public universities and K-12 school districts), `401(a) Mandatory / Optional Retirement Program (ORP)` (where state university faculty elect between a state defined-benefit pension and a defined-contribution the carrier annuity plan), `457(b) Governmental Deferred Compensation`, `457(b) Tax-Exempt Top-Hat Plan` (for highly compensated hospital/university executives), `457(f) Non-Qualified Executive Plan`, or `401(k) Corporate Plan`. The entity governs plan-level operational guardrails including `Default Investment Option (QDIA) Fund Identifier` (Department of Labor Qualified Default Investment Alternative, typically a Nuveen Target Date Lifecycle fund), SECURE 2.0 `Auto-Enrollment Enabled Indicator` and `Auto-Escalation Default Percentage`, `Plan Loan Permitted Indicator`, `Maximum Active Loans Allowed`, and `Hardship Withdrawal Permitted Indicator`. Furthermore, `Plan Eligibility & Contribution Rule` defines class-specific waiting periods, mandatory employee contributions, and employer base/matching formulas (`Employer Base Contribution Percentage`, `Employer Matching Formula Description`), while `Plan Vesting Schedule` enforces ERISA cliff or graded vesting rules (`Vesting Schedule Type`, `Years of Service Required for Full Vesting`, `Forfeiture Reallocation Rule`) on employer-funded money sources.

### 1.13 Domain 13: Underwriting & New Business

The **Underwriting & New Business** domain orchestrates the pre-issue acquisition funnel, transforming a prospect's initial financial quote or sales illustration into a formally submitted application, evaluating mortality and financial risk, and approving the contract for placement in force. Across the US Life and Annuity industry, new business acquisition bifurcates into two distinct operational tracks: (1) high-velocity, Guaranteed Issue or simplified-enrollment workflows for institutional retirement plan enrollments and retail deferred annuities, and (2) rigorous mortality, medical, and financial underwriting workflows for individual Term, Whole, Universal, Indexed Universal, and Variable Universal Life insurance policies. This domain unifies both tracks across four core logical entities: `Quote & Policy Illustration`, `Application`, `Underwriting Case & Risk Decision`, and `Medical & Mortality Requirement`.

`Quote & Policy Illustration` (`Illustration Identifier`, `Illustration Number`) captures the pre-sale ledger projection generated by the advisor (`Producer Identifier`) for a proposed `Product Identifier`. For life insurance and fixed/indexed annuities, NAIC Life Insurance Illustrations Model Regulation and Actuarial Guideline 49-A (AG49-A for IULs) strictly regulate how carriers illustrate `Guaranteed Values Projection Summary` (using guaranteed minimum crediting floors and maximum guaranteed Cost of Insurance charges) alongside `Non-Guaranteed Assumed Crediting Rate` projections, requiring a verified `Signed Illustration Received Indicator` matching the application at issue.

`Application` (`Application Identifier`, `Application Number`) records the formal request to purchase a policy or enroll in a plan (`Application Type`: `New Institutional Plan Enrollment`, `New Retail Cash Purchase`, `IRC 1035 Tax-Free Exchange (External Carrier)`, `Direct Qualified Plan-to-IRA Rollover`, `Internal Contract Replacement / Exchange`), ingested via digital platforms (`Application Channel` such as FireLight, AnnuityNet, iPipeline iGO, or Institutional Participant Web Enrollment). It tracks `Requested Face Amount`, `Planned Initial Contribution Amount`, the critical regulatory `Existing Insurance Replacement Indicator` (which triggers state Replacement Notices and heightened suitability scrutiny), `Electronic Signature Status`, and end-to-end `Application Status` (`Received - In Good Order (IGO)`, `Not In Good Order (NIGO - Pending Cure)`, `In Suitability & Compliance Review`, `In Medical Underwriting`, `Awaiting 1035 / Rollover Transfer Funds`, `Issued & Placed In Force`, `Withdrawn by Applicant`, `Declined`). For life and medically underwritten contracts, `Underwriting Case & Risk Decision` (`Underwriting Case Identifier`, `Underwriting Method`) manages risk triage—from `Guaranteed Issue` and `Accelerated Fluidless Algorithmic Underwriting` (using real-time predictive models) to `Full Traditional Medical & Financial Underwriting`—assigning the final `Assigned Mortality Risk Classification`, `Substandard Table Rating Factor` (e.g., Table B = 150%, Table D = 200% mortality), and `Flat Extra Premium Per Thousand Amount`. Supporting evidence items ordered during underwriting are tracked in `Medical & Mortality Requirement` (`Requirement Type`: MIB checks, Milliman Intelliscript / ExamOne Rx prescription histories, Motor Vehicle Reports, paramedical exams, blood/cotinine labs, and Attending Physician Statements [APS] / Electronic Health Records [EHR]).

### 1.14 Domain 14: Claims, Annuitization & Benefit Payouts

The **Claims, Annuitization & Benefit Payouts** domain governs the decumulation, retirement income distribution, and benefit settlement phase of the Life and Annuity value chain—the ultimate moment of truth where the insurance carrier fulfills its long-term contractual promises to retirees, annuitants, and grieving beneficiaries. Taking deep architectural inspiration from the carrier's century-long leadership in guaranteed lifetime income alongside broad US retail life and annuity claim standards, this domain is organized across four core logical entities: `Annuitization Payout Schedule`, `Required Minimum Distribution Schedule`, `Claim & Benefit Settlement`, and `Beneficiary Claim Payout Election`.

`Annuitization Payout Schedule` (`Annuitization Payout Schedule Identifier`, `Payout Contract Number`) governs the income phase when a policyholder elects to convert accumulated savings (or purchase a Single Premium Immediate Annuity) into a structured payout stream (`Annuitization Effective Date`, `First Income Payment Date`). It links the originating `Policy Identifier` to the measuring life (`Primary Annuitant Party Identifier`) and optional spouse/partner (`Joint Survivor Annuitant Party Identifier`), defining the actuarial `Payout Option Type`: `Single Life Annuity (Life Only)`, `Single Life with 10/15/20-Year Guaranteed Period Certain`, `Joint and 100% / 75% / Two-Thirds (66.7%) / 50% to Survivor`, `Transfer Payout Annuity (TPA 10-Year Fixed Installment)`, `Interest-Only Payout Option (IPRO)`, or an institutional `Retirement Transition Benefit` (allowing up to 10% lump-sum cash at annuitization). Crucially, it separates the `Guaranteed Minimum Periodic Payment Amount` (backed by contractual guaranteed interest and mortality tables) from the `Current Variable or Additional Dividend Payment Amount` (driven by Board-declared payout dividends or CREF variable annuity unit revaluations under the elected `Assumed Investment Return (AIR) Election`).

For active qualified contracts (403(b), 401(a), 401(k), 457(b), Traditional IRAs), `Required Minimum Distribution Schedule` (`RMD Schedule Identifier`) enforces IRS Internal Revenue Code Section 401(a)(9) and SECURE 2.0 Act mandates (age 73 / 75 thresholds), dividing the `Prior Year December 31 Fair Market Value` (plus any `Actuarial Present Value of Additional Plan Benefits` required under Treasury Reg. 1.401(a)(9)-6) by the divisor from `Applicable IRS Life Expectancy Table` (Table III Uniform Lifetime, Table II Joint Life for spouses >10 years younger, or Table I Single Life) to track `Calculated Annual RMD Amount`, `Satisfied RMD Amount Year-to-Date`, `Remaining RMD Amount Due`, and automated Minimum Distribution Option (`Automated RMD Payout Election Indicator`) sweeps. Finally, `Claim & Benefit Settlement` (`Claim Identifier`, `Claim Number`, `Claim Type`) manages Death Benefit claims, Living Benefit rider triggers, Disability Waivers, and Maturity settlements—tracking `Date of Loss or Death`, 2-year contestability verification, and the exact settlement formula (`Base Contract Benefit Amount` + `Guaranteed Minimum Death Benefit (GMDB) Step-Up Amount` + statutory `Delayed Settlement Interest Credited Amount` - `Less Outstanding Policy Loan Balance` = `Net Payable Claim Benefit Amount`)—while `Beneficiary Claim Payout Election` records each beneficiary's `Share Percentage Paid` and `Elected Settlement Method` (Lump Sum, Spousal Continuation, 10-Year SECURE Act Inherited Stretch, or Life Income Settlement).

### 1.15 Domain 15: Billing, Remittance & Tax Reporting

The **Billing, Remittance & Tax Reporting** domain manages the operational cash collection rails for institutional employer payroll feeds and retail recurring premiums, alongside the statutory federal and state income tax withholding and annual IRS information reporting engine. In a hybrid institutional-and-retail L&A organization like Corebridge, Prudential, Empower, or Principal, premium inflows arrive through two vastly different mechanisms: high-volume institutional employer payroll remittance batches covering thousands of university or hospital employees simultaneously, and individual retail billing schedules (monthly EFT bank drafts, quarterly/annual bills, or flexible lump sums). At the other end of the lifecycle, every distribution, rollover, loan default, and annuity payment must be subjected to precise tax withholding and reported to the Internal Revenue Service and state revenue departments. This domain is structured across three core logical entities: `Institutional Payroll Remittance Batch`, `Billing & Tax Withholding Schedule`, and `Tax Reporting Statement`.

`Institutional Payroll Remittance Batch` (`Remittance Batch Identifier`, `Remittance Batch Number`) tracks the bi-weekly, semi-monthly, or monthly payroll contribution cycles submitted by an `Employer Sponsor Organization` for an `Institutional Plan Identifier`. Under Department of Labor (DOL) ERISA fiduciary rules, employee salary deferrals deducted from paychecks (`Payroll Pay Period End Date`) must be remitted to the carrier and invested as soon as reasonably possible. The entity reconciles the structured payroll data file (`Remittance File Received Date`) against the incoming cash wire or ACH settlement (`Wire or ACH Settlement Date`), breaking down batch totals across `Total Employee Pre-Tax Contribution Amount`, `Total Employee Roth After-Tax Amount`, `Total Employer Matching Contribution Amount`, `Total Employer Basic Contribution Amount`, and `Total Loan Repayment Amount`. Only when `Batch Reconciliation Status` reaches `Cash & Data Balanced - Posted to Participant Policies` are individual participant `Financial Transaction` rows released to buy units at the market close `Valuation Price Date` (otherwise held in `Out-of-Balance Suspense Exception`).

At the individual policy level, `Billing & Tax Withholding Schedule` (`Billing & Withholding Schedule Identifier`) governs recurring modal premium billing (`Billing Mode`, `Billed Modal Premium Amount`, `Next Bill Due Date`, and contractual `Grace Period Expiration Date`—typically 31 days for traditional life or 61 days for universal life before lapse) alongside standing tax withholding elections: `Federal Income Tax Withholding Election Type` (distinguishing IRS Form W-4P wage-table withholding for periodic annuity payments, IRS Form W-4R flat-rate withholding with a 10% default for non-periodic withdrawals, and mandatory 20% withholding on eligible rollover distributions paid directly to a participant), `Federal Withholding Percentage or Dollar Amount`, and `State Income Tax Withholding Percentage or Dollar Amount`. Finally, `Tax Reporting Statement` (`Tax Statement Identifier`, `Tax Year`, `Tax Form Type`) generates annual IRS filings—most notably **Form 1099-R** (`Gross Distribution Amount (Box 1)`, `Taxable Amount (Box 2a)`, `Taxable Amount Not Determined Indicator` Box 2b, `Federal Income Tax Withheld (Box 4)`, after-tax `Employee After-Tax Contributions or Cost Basis Recovered (Box 5)`, and `IRS Distribution Code (Box 7)` such as `7`, `1`, `2`, `4`, `G`, `6`, `L`, `Q`), **Form 5498** (`Rollover Contributions Amount`, `December 31 Fair Market Value`), **Form 1099-INT** (delayed claim settlement interest), and **Form 1042-S** (non-resident alien distributions).

### 1.16 Domain 16: Compliance, Suitability & Regulatory Governance

The **Compliance, Suitability & Regulatory Governance** domain enforces the legal, fiduciary, consumer-protection, and market-conduct guardrails that govern the sale, post-issue cancellation, and unclaimed-property administration of Life Insurance and Annuity contracts across all 50 US states and federal regulatory regimes. Because annuities and permanent life insurance are complex, long-term financial instruments carrying surrender charges, market risk, or mortality fees, they are regulated by an overlapping mosaic of authorities: the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) and FINRA for registered variable and RILA products, the Department of Labor (DOL) for ERISA retirement plans and rollovers, and state Departments of Insurance (modeled on NAIC standards or stringent state-specific statutes like New York DFS). This domain is modeled across three core logical entities: `Suitability & Best Interest Review`, `Free-Look & Rescission Tracking`, and `Unclaimed Property & Escheatment Case`.

`Suitability & Best Interest Review` (`Compliance Review Identifier`, linked to `Application Identifier` and `Producer Identifier`) records the mandatory pre-issue supervisory compliance evaluation required before a recommended contract can be issued. It identifies the governing `Regulatory Framework`—such as `SEC Regulation Best Interest (Reg BI - Variable Products)`, `NAIC Suitability in Annuity Transactions Model Regulation #275 (Best Interest)` (adopted across nearly all US states), `New York Insurance Regulation 187 (Life & Annuity Best Interest)` (which uniquely extends strict best-interest rules to both life insurance and annuities in NY), or `DOL ERISA PTE 2020-02 Rollover Fiduciary Advice`—and captures the customer's Consumer Profile Information: `Customer Risk Tolerance`, `Customer Financial Objectives`, `Customer Investment Time Horizon`, `Existing Liquid Net Worth Amount`, and `Source of Funds`. When an application involves an external 1035 exchange or rollover replacement, compliance engines evaluate `1035 Replacement Surrender Penalty Incurred Amount` and compute a `Replacement Benefit Comparison Score` to prevent illegal "churning" or "twisting," requiring sign-off from a FINRA Series 24 / Series 26 principal (`Supervisory Principal Name`, `Compliance Review Decision`).

Immediately following policy issuance, `Free-Look & Rescission Tracking` (`Free-Look Tracking Identifier`) monitors the statutory post-delivery examination window starting on `Policy Delivery Date` and running for `State Mandated Free-Look Period Days` (typically 10 days for standard policies, but extended to 30 or 60 days for replacements or senior citizens aged 60+/65+ in states like California and New York) until `Free-Look Expiration Date`. If the owner exercises their right to cancel (`Free-Look Exercised Indicator = True`), `Refund Basis Rule` dictates whether the carrier refunds `Return of Full Gross Premium Paid` (where the carrier absorbs any interim market loss) or `Return of Current Account Value Plus Deducted Fees`, rescinding the contract from inception and triggering a 100% producer commission chargeback. Finally, `Unclaimed Property & Escheatment Case` (`Escheatment Case Identifier`) enforces NCOIL Unclaimed Life Insurance Benefits Act and state Revised Uniform Unclaimed Property Act (RUUPA) mandates—monitoring automated `Social Security Death Master File (DMF) Fuzzy/Exact Match` sweeps, `Returned Undeliverable Mail (RPO)`, and uncashed checks (`Trigger Source`), executing statutory due diligence outreach (`Good Faith Outreach Due Diligence Status`), and remitting unclaimed funds (`Escheatable Amount`) to the `Target Escheatment State Jurisdiction` after `State Dormancy Period Years` (typically 3 to 5 years).

---



## 2. Business Context Ontology Rules (Domains 1, 2, and 3)



### 2.1 Domain 1: Product (`BC-001` to `BC-020`)



#### BC-001: Every Product Must Belong to Exactly One Executive and Statutory Product Line

**Domains**: Product | **Entities**: `Product Line`, `Product` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Line Identifier`, `Line of Business Code`, `Product Line Name`, `Regulatory Tax Qualification Type`, `Target Market Segment`, `Product Identifier`

In an enterprise Life and Annuity insurance organization, no marketable `Product` can exist as an ungrouped orphan in the product catalog; every record in the `Product` entity must link via a mandatory foreign key (`Product Line Identifier`) to a single parent `Product Line`. This structural hierarchy reflects how US insurance carriers—such as Corebridge, Prudential, MetLife, New York Life, Lincoln Financial, and Nationwide—organize their commercial operations, statutory NAIC Annual Statement ("Blue Book") reporting lines, and executive profit-and-loss (P&L) centers. The `Product Line` entity establishes the macro-level classification through `Line of Business Code` (such as `IND-LIFE` for Individual Life, `GRP-ANN` for Group Annuities, `RTL-DEF-ANN` for Retail Deferred Annuities, `INST-RET-ANN` for Institutional Retirement Annuities, and `IMM-PAY-ANN` for Immediate Payout Annuities), alongside the overarching `Regulatory Tax Qualification Type` (e.g., `IRC 403(b) Tax-Sheltered Annuity`, `IRC 401(a) Institutional Plan`, `IRC 457(b) Deferred Compensation`, `Traditional IRA`, `Roth IRA`, or `Non-Qualified After-Tax`) and `Target Market Segment` (`Higher Education & Research`, `Healthcare & Non-Profit`, `K-12 Public Education`, `Retail Wealth Management`, `High Net Worth Estate Planning`). When individual contract designs in `Product` inherit this parent linkage, financial controllers and actuaries can seamlessly aggregate policy liabilities, premium inflows, and surrender outflows into statutory reporting lines (such as NAIC Summary of Operations lines for Individual Life vs. Group Annuities) and market segment dashboards. When AI agents generate executive summary reports, statutory line-of-business rollups, or segment profitability data products, they must always join `Policy` to `Product` on `Product Identifier` and then join `Product` to `Product Line` on `Product Line Identifier`, using `Line of Business Code` and `Target Market Segment` as the authoritative top-level grouping dimensions rather than attempting to parse substring patterns from `Product Code` or `Product Name`.

#### BC-002: Fundamental Actuarial and Balance Sheet Distinction Across Annuity and Life Product Categories

**Domains**: Product, Fund, Finance | **Entities**: `Product`, `General Ledger Account` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Identifier`, `Product Category`, `General or Separate Account Indicator`, `Separate vs General Account Ledger Code`

Every record in the `Product` entity is classified by `Product Category` and `General or Separate Account Indicator`, which together govern how policyholder assets are invested, who bears capital market and interest rate risk, and how liabilities sit on the insurer's balance sheet. In `General Account Fixed Annuity` products (such as Participating General Account Fixed, Multi-Year Guaranteed Annuities [MYGAs] from Athene or Symetra, and traditional fixed annuities), `General or Separate Account Indicator` is set to `General Account Only`, meaning all premiums flow into the insurer's unified General Account portfolio of corporate bonds, commercial mortgages, and private placements, and the carrier guarantees principal plus a contractual floor interest rate. Conversely, in `Variable Annuity` and `Variable Universal Life (VUL)` products (such as CREF Variable Retirement Annuities or Jackson National and Equitable variable annuities), assets are held in insulated sub-accounts (`Separate Account Only`) where the policyholder bears direct equity/bond market performance risk and assets are legally segregated from the insurer's general creditors. Between these poles sit `Hybrid General and Separate Account` designs, including `Fixed Indexed Annuity (FIA)` products (where funds reside in the General Account and earn index-linked interest via option budgets with a 0% floor), `Registered Index-Linked Annuity (RILA)` products (structured with buffers or floors of -10% or -20% over an index term), `Single Premium Immediate Annuity (SPIA)` / `Deferred Income Annuity (DIA)` payout contracts, and permanent life designs (`Whole Life`, `Universal Life`, `Indexed Universal Life (IUL)`). AI agents building balance sheet risk, Asset-Liability Management (ALM), or capital-efficiency data products must never commingle `General Account Only` balances (which drive spread income and C-1/C-3 statutory capital requirements) with `Separate Account Only` balances (which drive fee-based M&E revenue). Agents must explicitly group or filter by `Product Category` and `General or Separate Account Indicator` whenever reporting carrier balance sheet exposure versus third-party assets under administration.

#### BC-003: General Account Institutional Contract Variants (RA, GRA, RC, SRA, GSRA, RCP) Have Distinct Liquidity and Ownership Mechanics

**Domains**: Product, Policy, Institutional Plan & Employer Sponsor | **Entities**: `Product`, `Product Crediting & Liquidity Rule`, `Policy` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Code`, `Product Name`, `Lump Sum Withdrawal Allowed Indicator`, `Required Installment Payout Period Months`, `Early Withdrawal Penalty Percentage`

Within an institutional retirement data model for institutional and retail retirement carriers, AI agents must never treat "Participating General Account Fixed" as a single monolithic product with uniform withdrawal rules; instead, each institutional contract form in `Product` (`Product Code` and `Product Name`) links to vastly different liquidity and ownership regimes in `Product Crediting & Liquidity Rule`. The flagship `Participating General Account Retirement Annuity (RA)` (`Product Code = GA-RA`) is an individual ownership contract designed purely for lifetime retirement income: `Lump Sum Withdrawal Allowed Indicator` is `False (Installments Only)`, meaning principal can never be cashed out in a single lump sum during accumulation, and transfers or withdrawals out of Institutional RA must be staged over 10 annual payments (`Required Installment Payout Period Months = 120`) via a Transfer Payout Annuity (TPA). By contrast, the `Group Retirement Annuity (GRA)` (`GA-GRA`) permits lump-sum withdrawals only within 120 days of termination of employment subject to a 2.50% surrender fee (`Early Withdrawal Penalty Percentage = 0.0250`), or penalty-free over 10 annual TPA installments. Newer institutional designs—`Institutional Retirement Choice (RC)` (`GA-RC`), `Retirement Choice Plus (RCP)`, `Supplemental Retirement Annuity (SRA)`, and `Group Supplemental Retirement Annuity (GSRA)`—govern employer-controlled vs. voluntary supplemental plans: `GA-RC` permits withdrawals and transfers in 84 monthly installments (`Required Installment Payout Period Months = 84`) or a lump sum within 120 days of employment termination with a 2.50% surrender charge, whereas `SRA`, `GSRA`, and `RCP` contracts offer full lump-sum liquidity (`Lump Sum Withdrawal Allowed Indicator = True (Unrestricted Lump Sum)` and `Early Withdrawal Penalty Percentage = 0.0000`) in exchange for a lower guaranteed interest rate floor (typically 1.00% to 3.00% vs. higher total crediting rates on illiquid RA/GRA contracts). AI agents building liquidity risk models, participant rollover eligibility tools, or cash-flow projection engines must always join `Product` to `Product Crediting & Liquidity Rule` on `Product Identifier` to determine whether a policy's general account balance is liquid, subject to a 2.5% fee, or locked into 84-month or 120-month installment payouts.

#### BC-004: Dual-Regime Securities (SEC/FINRA) vs. State Department of Insurance Filing Governance

**Domains**: Product, Agent & Agency, Compliance, Suitability & Regulatory Governance | **Entities**: `Product`, `Product State Filing & Availability`, `Producer License & Carrier Appointment` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Category`, `State Filing Approval Number`, `Line of Authority`, `FINRA Series Qualification`

In the United States regulatory architecture, Life and Annuity products are governed by either a single-tier state insurance regulatory framework or a dual-tier federal securities AND state insurance framework, dictated by the `Product Category` in `Product`. Fixed products—including `General Account Fixed Annuity` (Participating General Account Fixed, MYGAs), `Fixed Indexed Annuity (FIA)` (exempt under the Harkin Amendment when meeting standard non-forfeiture laws), `Single Premium Immediate Annuity (SPIA)`, `Term Life`, `Whole Life`, `Universal Life`, and `Indexed Universal Life (IUL)`—are regulated exclusively as insurance contracts by state Departments of Insurance (DOIs). Conversely, `Variable Annuity`, `Registered Index-Linked Annuity (RILA)`, and `Variable Universal Life (VUL)` contracts are legally classified as both insurance contracts and federal securities registered with the US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) under the Securities Act of 1933 and/or the Investment Company Act of 1940 (with CREF uniquely registered as a management investment company). Despite SEC registration, variable and RILA policy forms must still be filed with and approved by every state DOI where sold, tracked in `Product State Filing & Availability` via `State Filing Approval Number`. This dual-regime classification cascades directly into distribution and compliance rules: to sell a fixed or indexed insurance product, a producer in `Producer License & Carrier Appointment` requires only a state `Line of Authority = Life Insurance Only`, whereas soliciting a Variable Annuity, RILA, or VUL legally requires `Line of Authority = Life and Variable Contracts` plus active FINRA broker-dealer registration (`FINRA Series Qualification` containing `Series 6` or `Series 7` and `Series 63`). AI agents building producer eligibility validation tools or compliance audit queries must cross-check `Product Category` against `Line of Authority` and `FINRA Series Qualification` to flag any unregistered solicitation of variable or RILA products as a severe FINRA/SEC regulatory violation.

#### BC-005: Interstate Insurance Product Regulation Commission (IIPRC) Compact vs. Non-Compact State Variations (New York DFS Regulation)

**Domains**: Product | **Entities**: `Product`, `Product State Filing & Availability` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Identifier`, `Issuing Legal Company Code`, `Jurisdiction State Name`, `State Filing Approval Number`, `State Variation Form Number`

When a US Life and Annuity carrier launches a new product design in `Product`, it cannot simply offer a single nationwide contract form without navigating state jurisdictional sovereignty captured in `Product State Filing & Availability`. Approximately 46 US states and territories participate in the Interstate Insurance Product Regulation Commission (IIPRC / "Interstate Compact"), allowing carriers to submit a unified product filing via SERFF (`State Filing Approval Number` prefixed with `IIPRC-`) that, once approved under uniform national standards, becomes valid across all participating Compact jurisdictions using a standard policy form (`State Variation Form Number`). However, several major insurance jurisdictions—most notably **New York** (regulated by the New York State Department of Financial Services [NY DFS]), **California**, **Florida**, and **South Carolina** (or states opting out of specific annuity/LTC compact standards)—require independent state-specific SERFF filings (`SERFF-`) with strict statutory modifications to non-forfeiture floors, surrender charge caps, free-look periods, and suicide contestability clauses (`State Variation Form Number` suffixed with `-NY`, `-CA`, or `-FL`). Furthermore, because New York Insurance Law Section 4228 and Regulation 187 impose extraterritorial expense limits and reserve rules on NY-domiciled insurers, national carrier groups (such as Lincoln Financial vs. Lincoln Life & Annuity Company of New York, Prudential vs. Pruco Life of New Jersey/NY, or Pacific Life vs. Pacific Life & Annuity Company) issue policies through distinct statutory legal entities tracked in `Issuing Legal Company Code` (whereas the carrier and CREF are uniquely domiciled directly in New York as `GA-NY-01` and `CREF-01`). AI agents querying product availability or validating new business applications must join `Product` to `Product State Filing & Availability` on `Product Identifier` and match the application's situs state against `Jurisdiction State Name`, verifying that `Approval Effective Date` is on or before the application signature date.

#### BC-006: Product Launch and Sunset Lifecycle — Open vs. Closed Blocks of Business

**Domains**: Product, Policy, Underwriting & New Business | **Entities**: `Product`, `Policy`, `Application` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Identifier`, `Effective Launch Date`, `Closure to New Business Date`, `Contract Issue Date`, `Submission Date`

Every contract specification in `Product` has a defined commercial solicitation window bounded by `Effective Launch Date` and `Closure to New Business Date`. When a carrier introduces a new product series (for example, transitioning from legacy 3.0% minimum floor annuities to updated 1.0%–3.0% floating floor designs, or launching a new CSO mortality table life series), `Effective Launch Date` marks the first calendar day on which quotes (`Quote & Policy Illustration`) and applications (`Application`) may be accepted. When interest rates shift, regulatory rules change (such as Principle-Based Reserving VM-20/VM-21 updates or SECURE 2.0 changes), or a product is superseded by a newer generation, the carrier sets `Closure to New Business Date` to a concrete calendar date (whereas currently marketed products have `Closure to New Business Date = Null (Open to New Business)`). Crucially, in Life and Annuity insurance, setting `Closure to New Business Date` **only stops the issuance of new policies** (`Application` records where `Submission Date > Closure to New Business Date` are prohibited, subject to brief regulatory pipeline transition windows); it **does not terminate or invalidate existing in-force policies** in the `Policy` entity! Millions of "closed-block" policies issued prior to `Closure to New Business Date` (such as legacy Institutional RA contracts from the 1980s and 1990s, or closed variable annuity blocks at Hartford/Talcott Resolution, Venerable, and Fortitude Re) remain `Active in Accumulation` or `Active in Payout / Annuitized` for decades, continuing to accept recurring payroll/modal premiums, accrue interest, and pay claims under their original grandfathered contract terms. AI agents must never filter out products where `Closure to New Business Date IS NOT NULL` when calculating total in-force AUM, statutory reserves, or active policyholder counts; that filter should only be applied when querying the active product catalog available for new sales illustrations and new business applications.

#### BC-007: Contractual Issue Age Boundaries (`Minimum Issue Age` and `Maximum Issue Age`) by Product Design

**Domains**: Product, Policy, Party, Customer & Household, Underwriting & New Business | **Entities**: `Product`, `Policy`, `Party`, `Application` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Identifier`, `Minimum Issue Age`, `Maximum Issue Age`, `Date of Birth`, `Contract Issue Date`

Every product in the `Product` entity enforces strict demographic entry boundaries through `Minimum Issue Age` and `Maximum Issue Age`, which constrain the age of the `Primary Insured` (for life insurance) or `Primary Annuitant` / `Policy Owner` (for annuities) on the `Contract Issue Date` (or `Submission Date` on `Application`). Depending on the state filing and actuarial mortality basis, carriers calculate issue age as either **Attained Age** (exact age on the last birthday prior to issue, standard across annuities and institutional retirement plans) or **Age Nearest Birthday** (the birthday within 6 months of issue, common in traditional Whole Life and Term Life policies). Juvenile whole life and indexed universal life products allow a `Minimum Issue Age` of `0` (typically 14 or 15 days old), workplace institutional retirement annuities (`GA-RA`, `GA-RC`, `CREF-PA`) typically set `Minimum Issue Age = 18`, and specialized longevity or GMWB income riders often require a `Minimum Issue Age` of `50` or `55`. At the upper boundary, `Maximum Issue Age` protects the carrier from adverse mortality/longevity selection and suitability violations against elderly consumers: 20-year or 30-year Term Life policies typically cap `Maximum Issue Age` at `65` or `70`; retail Variable and Fixed Indexed Annuities with 7-to-10-year surrender charges commonly cap `Maximum Issue Age` at `80` or `85` (often lower in states like California or New York under senior suitability guidelines); and Single Premium Immediate Annuities (`SPIA`) or institutional Required Minimum Distribution payout contracts allow `Maximum Issue Age` up to `90` or `95`. AI agents building pre-issue underwriting validation or data quality rules must verify that the insured/annuitant's age (derived from `Party.Date of Birth` and `Policy.Contract Issue Date`) falls inclusively between `Product.Minimum Issue Age` and `Product.Maximum Issue Age`.

#### BC-008: Minimum Initial Contribution Thresholds: Institutional Payroll Plans vs. Retail Lump-Sum Contracts

**Domains**: Product, Policy, Underwriting & New Business | **Entities**: `Product`, `Policy`, `Application` | **Key Attributes**: `Product Identifier`, `Minimum Initial Contribution Amount`, `Planned Initial Contribution Amount`, `Regulatory Tax Qualification Type`

The attribute `Minimum Initial Contribution Amount` on the `Product` entity defines the minimum dollar funding required to establish a new contract in good order (`Received - In Good Order (IGO)`), and its magnitude varies by several orders of magnitude depending on whether the product serves institutional workplace retirement plans or retail wealth management channels. In institutional workplace retirement plans—such as `IRC 403(b) Tax-Sheltered Annuity` and `IRC 401(a) Institutional Plan` offerings administered by Empower, Fidelity, or Corebridge for university staff, adjunct faculty, nurses, and K-12 teachers—participants fund their contracts incrementally through bi-weekly or monthly payroll deductions. Consequently, institutional products (`GA-RA`, `GA-GRA`, `GA-RC`, `CREF-PA`) set `Minimum Initial Contribution Amount` to a nominal threshold such as `$0.00` (allowing shell enrollment prior to the first payroll batch) or `$25.00` per payroll cycle so that every eligible employee can participate regardless of income level. Conversely, in retail wealth management channels—such as retail `Fixed Indexed Annuity (FIA)`, `Multi-Year Guaranteed Annuity (MYGA)`, `Registered Index-Linked Annuity (RILA)`, or `Single Premium Immediate Annuity (SPIA)` contracts distributed through broker-dealers like LPL Financial—the carrier incurs substantial upfront issue, underwriting, and commission expenses, requiring a `Minimum Initial Contribution Amount` of `$10,000.00`, `$25,000.00`, or `$50,000.00` (frequently with lower minimums of `$5,000.00` for qualified Traditional/Roth IRAs versus `$10,000.00` or `$25,000.00` for Non-Qualified lump sums, and `$100,000.00` or `$1,000,000.00` breakpoints for institutional or premier high-band crediting rate tiers). When AI agents evaluate new business pipeline conversion or validate `Application.Planned Initial Contribution Amount` against `Product.Minimum Initial Contribution Amount`, they must account for institutional payroll shell enrollments where initial cash arrives asynchronously via `Institutional Payroll Remittance Batch` rather than as a single check or wire accompanying the retail application.

#### BC-009: Surrender Charge Schedule Structures (CDSC) and Free Withdrawal Corridor Mechanics

**Domains**: Product, Policy, Fund, Transactions | **Entities**: `Product`, `Product Crediting & Liquidity Rule`, `Policy Valuation Account`, `Financial Transaction` | **Key Attributes**: `Surrender Charge Schedule Type`, `Early Withdrawal Penalty Percentage`, `Surrender Free Value Amount`, `Surrender Charge Deducted Amount`

To recover upfront distribution commissions and enable the insurer to invest General Account assets in longer-duration, higher-yielding fixed-income securities without sudden disintermediation risk, most deferred annuities and Universal Life policies impose a Contingent Deferred Sales Charge (CDSC), captured on `Product` via `Surrender Charge Schedule Type` and detailed in `Product Crediting & Liquidity Rule`.
